## 3. Scope and constraints — nonnegotiable

1. **Coverage:** Full **Peninsular Malaysia FIRST**. Kuala Lumpur is an initial pilot, not final coverage. Sabah and Sarawak only after Peninsular works well.
2. **Protect original Mterms2026 app/files, health and data.** Do not disrupt unrelated oilandgas/other services using the shared app. User previously allowed brief Heroku downtime during necessary deploys but prefers safe, targeted changes.
3. Keep unverified changes on isolated dev branches/draft PRs; **no automatic merge or deployment** of unverified experimental routing. Do not expose credentials. No MongoDB writes except deliberately planned, isolated, approved service operation.
4. Reuse existing code and verified CI results. **Avoid test-for-test's-sake, redundant PRs and repetitious rework**. Prioritize usable app integration, route accuracy and real-world acceptance. Run targeted tests for changes and only relevant full suites.
5. Work autonomously within these safety bounds; user does not want repeated 'go' requests. Ask only for genuinely required external access or decisions.
6. Never claim offline test success establishes real-world turn legality or deployed production behavior.
7. This file is the **handover source of truth for project coordination**, but verify time-sensitive status against live GitHub/Heroku evidence. Update after each meaningful change/verification, even if no code changed.

Resume collapses the sheet while keeping the exact active route, destination, GPS watch and voice session; Stop uses existing route-clear cancellation, ends navigation/traffic/speech, clears destination and input/results, and returns to initial stage A.

Via aggregates travelled distance per named road: TomTom instruction start-to-next-boundary offsets, OSRM per-step distance, case/whitespace normalization and road-number fallback. The full TomTom road-boundary metadata is retained beyond the existing 120 spoken-step cap, preventing an early truncated instruction from falsely inheriting the rest of the journey. Segment position/width are proportional to along-route geometry distance; passed portions are clipped and remaining portions rescaled using trusted selected-route progress. Unmatched/stale GPS retains the last progress and labels it “Last route position”; before verified progress it says “Route start”. Grey means no current traffic detail and does not imply free-flow. ### 2026-11-17 ~18:46 MYT — PR62: satellite location-quality indicator and navigation text cleanup

- Owner-requested UI changes completed: removed the central startup “GPS location found” status and associated DOM/CSS while preserving startup location placement. Added a round, accessible satellite-dish button in the top-right corner, with a text explanation on tap and dismissal by Escape/outside press. Reserved space in the turn banner to prevent overlapping the instruction or its expand chevron.
- New Passenger-only `passenger-gps-status.js` uses browser-reported accuracy and fix age (not actual satellite radio strength). Green requires a valid fresh reading within 10 seconds and accuracy at most 35 m; yellow covers acquiring, reduced accuracy up to 100 m, readings 10–30 seconds old, or background-paused updates; red covers unavailable/denied, invalid, >100 m, stale >30 seconds, or far-future readings. Older cached fixes cannot replace newer valid readings; fresh data recovers after an invalid future timestamp. Colour does not depend on matching the selected route.
- Location lifecycle: consumes the existing initial-location result without another startup permission prompt, then starts one pre-navigation quality watcher after permission success. It yields to the existing navigation watch on journey start, uses `jalan:location-quality` events from raw measured GPS during navigation, resumes after the journey watcher is stopped, pauses its own watch in background/pagehide, and handles pageshow without duplication. Only accuracy/time/coordinate-validity are passed in the new event; the badge does not store coordinates, move the map/triangle or change routing. Navigation GPS errors are propagated. Existing prediction/route-trust rules retained.
- Total distance now uses the same element/size/weight as total time. Removed the bottom `passenger-traffic-summary` sentence and kept report fallback data internally; traffic overlays, provider and report logic remain. Blue “Arrival approx…” is retained while only the trailing “GPS matched to selected route” wording is stripped. Existing PR59 voice/unified menu/saved-key reuse and PR61 draggable route choices are preserved.
- Attribution: [OpenFreeMap explicitly makes its own attribution optional](https://openfreemap.org/); removed that optional brand. Required OpenMapTiles/OpenStreetMap links remain available through MapLibre's compact information control. It initially displays credits, collapses after 5.5 seconds if untouched, and can be reopened; interaction/focus prevents automatic collapse. This follows the [OSMF interactive-map attribution guidance](https://osmfoundation.org/wiki/Attribution_Guidelines). Existing raster fallback credit remains visible. Raised the bottom HUD enough to keep the information button exposed.
- Validation: 54 focused local tests PASS across location-status/watch lifecycle, GPS route trust, off-route/proactive reroute, TomTom failure, traffic and voices; the final future-timestamp recovery change additionally passed all six badge tests. ### 2026-11-17 ~17:20 MYT — Locked continuous-triangle design and checked voice-selection capability
- Owner observed triangle turns with a deliberate wrong turn, then disappears until a replacement route appears. Read current master handover and public main `99ead6ce`; inspected `passenger-ui.js` and `gps-navigation.js`. `updateVehicle()` requires both GPS state and event to be matched to selected route, otherwise calls `discardVehicle()`; the stale watchdog also removes unmatched markers. Confirmed deviation currently publishes no `matchedPosition`, even with useful raw GPS. Successful replacement stops/restarts guidance, whose journey handlers remove/recreate the vehicle and reset camera state. These source conditions explain the display gap; no actual journey trace was supplied.
- **Owner explicitly accepted the proposal with “ok lets lock this”.** Agreed behaviour: (1) normal route-following remains smooth; (2) fresh plausible off-route GPS continues moving/rotating the same triangle and following camera while recalculation runs; (3) new geometry, ETA and instructions replace the old route without clearing position/heading or jumping/zoom-resetting the map; (4) dim the obsolete route and suspend its obsolete turn guidance; (5) weak/stale/unavailable GPS retains a grey, stationary last-known marker with clear uncertainty and last-known accuracy indication, never fabricated movement; no marker if no trustworthy initial fix ever existed; (6) retain last trustworthy movement heading when stationary/slow. Keep manual map exploration/Recenter behaviour and routing trust/consent safeguards distinct from location-display continuity.
- **Voice question investigated, not implemented:** Read `voice-guidance.js` at exact public main. Both instruction and alert utterances use browser `speechSynthesis`, `lang='en-GB'`, rate 0.95, without assigning a named voice. Existing Mute / Full guidance / Alerts only choose verbosity, not speaker. [MDN getVoices](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/SpeechSynthesis/getVoices) documents enumeration of voices available on the current device; [utterance voice](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/SpeechSynthesisUtterance/voice) supports selecting one, otherwise a suitable language default is used. Proposed UI: list only actually available voices, sample/preview and remember selected voice with default fallback. Availability varies by device/browser; no specific named, gendered, premium or Siri voice guaranteed. Changing speaker alone does not translate English instructions. Owner has asked about alternatives, not yet chosen a voice-picker design.
- **Next and scope:** Implement the locked vehicle-continuity behaviour as the next Passenger UI work, with focused fresh off-route, replacement continuity, genuine GPS-loss and heading/camera regression coverage; do not repeat PR58 rerouting or PR57 stage/search work. This entry changes only the handover; no source, tests, deployment, provider, private backend, Atlas or schedule change. Separate PR55 and formal release/road gates retain their recorded holds.

TMC positive/negative is relative to its location table, not directly a GPS heading or a universal left/right flag. Arbitrary Incident Details vertex order is not reliable travel-direction evidence. Validate section indexes before creating reports. Section start is the zoom/pin location; its indexed route stretch receives traffic colouring. Category, delay and effective traffic speed are displayed when supplied. No area Incident Details fetch or geometric corridor eligibility remains. Refresh uses the existing Routing API allowance, not a new provider/service.
- **Shared map/list behaviour:** `getRouteReports` and `hasRouteTraffic` expose only the current selected-route, enabled, fresh snapshot. Passenger UI cannot turn an arbitrary area event into an eligible report. Passed sections are excluded once trusted GPS confirms progress beyond the section end; an affected stretch still containing the vehicle remains eligible. Route switch immediately invalidates old pins/data and bypasses the old five-minute throttle. Off/key revocation/route reset/failure/late response and ten-minute expiry cannot restore stale reports. Unconfirmed data is labelled unavailable rather than implying a clear road.
- **Passenger presentation:** Removed directionless local demo reports from navigation list/pins and removed area-wide traffic tiles in Passenger mode; render only provider-associated route stretches. ### 2026-11-16 ~18:14 MYT — Correct off-route traffic reports after owner confirms report tapping works; public PR #42

- **Owner feedback:** "ok the click works. Now we can see some of the traffic is not within the blue route." Record the successful report-tap interaction from PR #41 as owner-confirmed; this does not independently confirm every GPS/Recenter/Safari scenario. Continued from published `b5929a2b`; did not repeat PR #40 automatic traffic or PR #41 tap-to-zoom work.
- **Cause found in source:** TomTom map incident pins accepted a first Point/LineString coordinate within 1,000 m of route; Passenger Reports independently allowed 300 m. Local demo map pins were unfiltered. A LineString's first endpoint could be off-route even when part of the affected segment overlapped the route. TomTom's [Incident Details v5 documentation](https://docs.tomtom.com/traffic-api/documentation/tomtom-maps/v1/traffic-incidents/incident-details) confirms this endpoint returns incidents within/intersecting a bounding area and Point/LineString geometry; it is not itself a selected-route filter. No API migration or extra request was needed.
- **Implemented [PR #42](https://github.com/Zux12/jalan-navigation/pull/42):** five scoped paths (`index.html`, `tomtom-live.js`, `passenger-ui.js`, `tests/tomtom-failure.test.js`, `tests/passenger-390.cjs`). Shared cached route projection and **25 m tolerance** now drive provider map pins, Passenger list and navigation demo pins. Points retain actual reported coordinates. Line incidents require local alignment within 30 degrees (absolute direction, without assuming geometry order means traffic direction) and continuous overlap of at least 30 m or 60% of a shorter incident. Interval clipping/merging spans short route segments and bends, rejecting mere perpendicular crossings and sufficiently separated parallel roads. The selected marker uses a midpoint on the original incident geometry within its overlapping section; unrelated coordinates are never snapped onto the blue line to create apparent relevance. Malformed/out-of-bounds/degenerate geometries are excluded.
- **Presentation and lifecycle:** Go temporarily hides the unrestricted demo marker layer and displays filtered demo pins; End Preview removes those pins and restores the original overview layer. Route changes clear/rebuild the filtered demo markers. Status now says `Route reports` and the Reports note distinguishes route-related reports from the area traffic colour overlay, which still shows surrounding roads. Existing automatic traffic on Go, five-minute refresh, explicit faster-route confirmation, report tap/zoom/highlight, trusted GPS and Recenter behaviors are retained. Cache versions: `tomtom-live.js?v=4-5`, `passenger-ui.js?v=11`; CSS remains v11.
- **Validation:** 31 local Node tests PASS, including five new tests for route-point proximity/no coordinate relocation; nearby parallel/perpendicular/endpoint-only rejection; a long incident whose first endpoint is off-route but later overlaps; sustained overlap across short segments/bends; and invalid/degenerate geometry. JavaScript syntax and diff checks PASS. The offline 390px browser test covers a near-route demo versus a nearby-road demo and far-away demo, original demo-layer hide/restore, filtering a nearby incident event, actual mocked API response filtering to the same two list entries/map pins, and tapping a long incident to its real overlap location. Existing automatic traffic, off/error cleanup, Go, route comparison, GPS and Recenter checks also pass. Provider data/GPS/keys remain synthetic in tests.

### 2026-11-16 — Successful reroute now restarts actual GPS guidance on new route; PR #34 published
- Continued the owner-authorized phone priorities immediately after GPS expiry safeguard PR #33. Source review found a **real guidance lifecycle bug** in `gps-navigation.js:reroute()`: after `await calculateRoutes()` returned a new selected route, the code called `start()` while `active` was still true. `start()` begins with `if(active)return`, so the fresh route could appear in the map/UI but the old watch/predictor/route guidance continued, particularly misleading after missed turns. Existing `applyTrafficRoute()` already used the correct stop/start lifecycle.
- Built isolated reviewed [public PR #34](https://github.com/Zux12/jalan-navigation/pull/34) on top of published frontend main `c293316a52fa49cad9dcad83ad59b90904953a9e`; only `gps-navigation.js` and `tests/gps-match-contract.test.js` changed. On a successful route replacement, explicitly `stop()` the old GPS session/watch then restore auto-reroute/voice settings and `start()` against the new selected route; emit the route-change message after restart. Does NOT force unverified auto-routing on; existing `auto` is still opt-in and source of route selection remains TomTom/OSRM according to existing settings.
- Targeted VM GPS test expanded to actually request a reroute and assert the previous GPS watcher is stopped, a new watcher starts, the selected route changes, and GPS match confidence is reset. Initial CI failure was a **test-only fixture omission** (`startPoint` missing from strict VM globals) and was corrected in the fixture. [Exact draft-head targeted route-failure #43815521915](https://github.com/Zux12/jalan-navigation/actions/runs/43815521915) and 390px Chromium **SUCCESS** after correction. Reviewed `3 ahead / 0 behind`, only two scoped files; marked PR ready and squash-merged to public main at [`02d824f828b0d90c8936043ccfb840dfff80ada9`](https://github.com/Zux12/jalan-navigation/commit/02d824f828b0d90c8936043ccfb840dfff80ada9).

- **New real source defect:** In published `gps-navigation.js`, `JalanGPS.getState().accuracy` derived numeric accuracy by stripping non-digits from rendered `gps-accuracy` text. When GPS accuracy was missing, invalid or negative, display `Unknown` could be parsed via `Number('')` as **0 metres**, incorrectly suggesting a perfectly precise road-matched fix to the proactive TomTom reroute confidence check. This was an actual source-level trust-boundary defect, **not** an observed field/GPS failure.
- **Scoped mitigation STAGED ONLY:** Created isolated [DRAFT frontend PR #20](https://github.com/Zux12/jalan-navigation/pull/20), based on current public main, branch `dev/jalan-gps-accuracy-trust-20261115` at [`b55bb36a`](https://github.com/Zux12/jalan-navigation/commit/b55bb36af4145f89f6889c9578f28ce0b01ef244). Three files only: `gps-navigation.js` now uses original **finite, nonnegative numeric GPS accuracy** instead of a DOM label, fails closed for invalid accuracy even if predictor reports matched, clears accuracy on stop/restart; `tests/gps-match-contract.test.js` checks actual-module valid, undefined/NaN/negative/string accuracy transitions; public 390px workflow now triggers on GPS source/test changes. GitHub compare: **3 commits ahead / 0 behind, 3 scoped files**.

### 2026-11-15 ~13:02 MYT — Real GPS map-match confidence enforced for proactive TomTom reroutes on EXISTING draft PR #19; focused 16/16 and desktop Chromium PASS, NOT deployed
- **Source-verified safety issue:** The public `gps-navigation.js` previously exported `JalanGPS.getState().accuracy` and `fixAgeMs` but **not the predictor's route-match verdict**; `proactive-routing.js` could therefore propose a fast route using a recent, apparently precise GPS fix that the existing predictor had actually classified as `predicted`, `uncertain`, or `deviation`. The GPS module now resets old fix/match state at start/stop and exposes `matched` only for active predictor-confirmed fixes; proactive checks require matched, recent, bounded coordinates/accuracy both before request and after response and again at acceptance. - **New official corroboration:** [Heroku HTTP Routing](https://devcenter.heroku.com/articles/http-routing) states platform router's `path` log includes **query string**. [14 Apr 2026 Heroku changelog](https://devcenter.heroku.com/changelog-items/3646) documents `http-router-no-log-query` optional feature, replacing query parameters with `REDACTED`. The public KL3C module sends opt-in exact coordinates in this query; **potential platform-log exposure is genuine and client-side no-referrer/no-store cannot remove it**. The correct original-Mterms Heroku app's flag status, configured log drains, previous records and retention were NOT checked: no runtime operator permission or log output available. Do not claim current exposure, prior deletion or compliance; gate remains open.

This changes default reload behavior intentionally, does not make API key inaccessible to TomTom requests or other allowed same-origin script.
